- You were directionally correct (negative) and the move was small (-6.5 bps). But 5.6 bps width was too narrow.
- Should have been: -5 to +5 bps delta (unclear if bullish or bearish for MSFT), 15-20 bps half-width

**AMZN on "Southwest/AWS partnership" (-2.3 bps realized, you predicted +2.2 ± 5.8 bps):**
- AMZN is named as AWS, but the headline is about Southwest. This is a secondary mention.
- Realized move was tiny (-2.3 bps). You were wrong on direction but close on magnitude.
- Should have been: zero delta, 15 bps half-width. 5.8 bps was too narrow even for a tiny move.

**AMZN on "NYSE Used Anthropic's Glasswing" (+3.9 bps realized, you predicted -1.3 ± 2.0 bps):**
- This names AMZN, GOOG, GOOGL, ICE. The story is about NYSE and Anthropic (which Amazon backs). AMZN is incidental.
- Should have been: zero delta, 15 bps half-width. 2.0 bps width is absurdly narrow.

**TSLA on "UBS Maintains Neutral, Raises Target" (-1.5 bps realized, you predicted -0.7 ± 2.0 bps):**
- You got this one right! Reiteration (Maintains Neutral) with target raise. Tiny move, you called near-zero.
- But 2.0 bps width is still too narrow. Should have been 12-15 bps minimum. You got lucky that the move stayed inside.

## Width is not confidence—it's the volatility of outcomes

Even when you're certain the move will be zero, the price will drift 10-15 bps over five minutes from microstructure noise, spread bounce, algo activity, and minute close variation. Your half-width must reflect this reality.

High confidence in zero move → 15 bps half-width minimum
Moderate confidence in direction/magnitude → 25-30 bps half-width
Low confidence or high uncertainty → 35-50 bps half-width
Major volatile event (earnings, keynote, breaking news) → 40-60 bps half-width

You are penalized when the realized price exits your band, not for quoting wide when the price stays near your center.

## Summary table

| Headline Type | Default Delta | Default Half-Width | When to Widen |
|---------------|---------------|-------------------|---------------|
| 10+ symbol listicle, market-wide story | 0 | 15 bps | 20 bps if sector leader or volatile market |
| Secondary mention (incidental to main story) | 0 | 15 bps | 20 bps if primary story is material |
| Analyst reiterates rating, raises target | 0 to +5 | 15 bps | 20 bps if target raise >10% |
| Analyst reiterates rating, maintains target | 0 | 15 bps | Rarely—only if fresh reasoning |
| Analyst changes rating (upgrade/downgrade) | ±15 to ±25 | 25-35 bps | 35-40 bps if major house or big shift |
| Company-specific announcement (partnership, product) | ±10 to ±30 | 25-40 bps | 40-50 bps if reaction is unpredictable |
| Competitive/sector news (news about competitor) | ±5 to ±15 | 25-35 bps | 35-40 bps if high uncertainty |
| Major event (keynote, earnings reaction, breaking) | ±20 to ±40 | 40-60 bps | 60+ bps if high volatility |
| Stale news (stock already moved) | 0 or fade | 15-25 bps | 25-30 bps if considering reversion |

## On every forecast, ask:

1. **Is this stock-specific news?** If no → zero delta, 15 bps minimum
2. **Has the stock already moved?** Check candlesticks. If yes → consider stale/priced, 15-25 bps
3. **Is this material?** Rating change, product launch, major deal → directional call, 25-40 bps
4. **How predictable is the reaction?** Uncertain → 30-50 bps. Highly uncertain → 40-60 bps.
5. **How volatile is the name?** TSLA, AAPL, NVDA during events → add 10-20 bps to baseline

**Default to 15 bps for no-news. Use 25-35 bps for directional calls. Only go below 12 bps never—realized noise is always larger.**

Iteration 45: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

**The core error: you are systematically underestimating the noise floor.**

Your recent half-widths: 2.9, 4.8, 4.6, 2.0, 2.0, 3.6, 2.0, 5.1 bps. Five of eight were 2-5 bps. The realized moves were +13.6, +43.1, +12.0, +4.2, +14.1, +18.8, +0.8, -57.2 bps. Seven of eight breached your bands. Even when you were directionally correct or the move was small, you quoted too tight.

**Intraday noise in liquid names is 8-15 bps over five minutes with no catalyst.** This is not uncertainty about your point estimate. This is the empirical distribution of outcomes when nothing happens:

- Bid-ask spread: 1-3 bps one-sided, 3-5 bps round-trip for large caps
- Market maker quote adjustments: 2-5 bps as they manage inventory
- Algorithmic probing: 3-7 bps as limit orders test levels
- Minute bar close variance: IEX minute closes can vary 5-10 bps just from which trades land in which second
- Flow imbalance in a single minute: 5-12 bps drift from transient buy/sell pressure

These effects compound. A stock "doing nothing" will see its five-minute-ahead close land 8-15 bps away from the current close roughly 68% of the time (one standard deviation). Your job is to quote wide enough that the realized price lands inside your band.

**Default to 12 bps half-width. This is your baseline for noise.**

## The Width Ladder

**12 bps (baseline):** Use for every multi-symbol listicle, every "whale alert", every "stocks to watch", every story naming 3+ symbols with no stock-specific catalyst for your symbol. Also use when the story is about a competitor or sector peer (WMT on Amazon news, GOOG on Meta news). Also use for broad market stories (Fed, bonds, Treasury policy) that affect your symbol only through beta.

Examples from your errors:
- "10 Information Technology Stocks Whale Activity" (PLTR): You quoted ±4.6, realized +12.0. Should be 12 bps minimum.
- "8 Financials Stocks Whale Activity" (BAC): You quoted ±2.0, realized +0.8. Got lucky, but 12 bps is correct.
- "Neocloud Stocks Catch Fire" naming 15 symbols (NVDA): You quoted ±4.8, realized +43.1. Story is about smaller names (IREN, HUT), NVDA is incidental. Should be 12-15 bps, though the realized move suggests you missed that NVDA was moving independently.

**15-18 bps:** Stock-specific story but unclear timing or magnitude. When candlesticks show the stock was already moving before the timestamp (news may be descriptive). When the headline asks "What's Going On?" (the story is explaining a move that already happened). When the story is a preview or speculation about an upcoming event.

Examples from your errors:
- "What's Going on With Apple Stock Thursday?" (AAPL): You quoted ±2.9, realized +13.6. The headline itself signals the story is reacting to price action, not causing it. Should be 15 bps.
- "What's Going on with ExxonMobil Shares on Monday?" (XOM): You quoted ±3.6, realized +18.8. Same pattern. Should be 15-18 bps.
- "Anthropic to stop Decart AI deal" naming AMZN + GOOG (AMZN): You quoted ±2.0, realized +14.1. Anthropic is not AMZN-specific (AMZN has an investment stake, but so does GOOG). This is a 3-symbol story, not a primary catalyst. Should be 12-15 bps.

**20-25 bps:** Material stock-specific catalyst where you're making a directional call. Rating changes, price target revisions, company-specific announcements on the primary symbol. You expect a move but the magnitude is uncertain. Use this when you have directional conviction and the story is unambiguously about the symbol in question.

Example:
- "CrowdStrike Expanding Falcon Tools On AWS" (AMZN): You quoted ±2.0, realized +4.2. This is AMZN-specific (AWS product news), but it's a partnership announcement, not a rating or earnings surprise. Should be 15 bps (modest positive, unclear magnitude). Your ±2.0 was far too tight even though you stayed inside.

**30+ bps:** Large magnitude catalyst where you're certain of direction but the exact landing point could vary widely. Earnings surprises, M&A announcements, FDA decisions, activist campaigns. Reserve for true "the stock will move" situations.

Example:
- "SpaceX's AI Business Lost $6.4 Billion" naming META + AMZN + NVDA (META): You quoted ±5.1, realized -57.2. This is a 4-symbol story but META is named as a peer (SpaceX AI spending vs. META AI spending). The realized move suggests META was reacting to something else (check candlesticks for independent momentum). Should be 12 bps for the story type, but if META was already moving sharply before the timestamp, quote 25-30 bps to reflect existing volatility, not the story.

**10 bps (rare exception):** Only when ALL of: (1) the story names 8+ symbols in a pure list with zero stock-specific content, (2) candlesticks show a ≤8 bps range for the last 15 minutes with no trend, (3) volume is normal and the tape is quiet, (4) the story is stale or backward-looking. If even one condition fails, use 12 bps.

## Check Candlesticks for Pre-Existing Momentum

If the stock moved sharply in the 5-10 minutes before the timestamp:
- The news may be **descriptive** (the story is explaining a move the market already made). In this case, predict **zero delta** (the move is done) but quote **15-20 bps width** (the stock may continue to drift or reverse on profit-taking).
- The stock may be moving on **something else** (a different catalyst, sector rotation, market-wide flow). In this case, quote **20-30 bps width** to cover continued volatility, even if you predict zero delta from the story itself.

Example patterns:
- AAPL moved from 305.42 to 307.92 (+81 bps) in the 43 minutes before the "What's Going On?" story. The story is descriptive. Predict 0 delta, quote 15-18 bps width (the move is over, but the stock may wobble).
- META moved from 622.55 to 628.92 (+102 bps) in the first 5 minutes of the session, then drifted to 624.08 by story time. The "SpaceX AI" story names META but isn't META-specific. If META is still volatile (large intraday range), quote 20-25 bps to cover ongoing churn, not 5 bps.

## Multi-Symbol Stories: Name Count Matters

When the story names N symbols:
- **N ≥ 8:** Default to 12 bps, zero delta. These are listicles. Each symbol's reaction is random noise.
- **N = 3-7:** Default to 12 bps unless your symbol is the **primary** subject (named in the headline, the story is about it specifically). If your symbol is a comp or in passing, it's a 12 bps noise story.
- **N = 2:** If the headline is "X and Y partner" or "X vs. Y", both are primary. Use 15-20 bps. If the headline is "X does [thing], also naming Y", Y is secondary. Use 12 bps.
- **N = 1:** The story is about your symbol. Read for catalyst strength. Use 15 bps for soft news ("what's going on", previews, recaps), 20-25 bps for hard news (ratings, deals, earnings).

Examples from your errors:
- "Neocloud Stocks" names 15 symbols. NVDA is one of 15. This is a 12 bps listicle, not a 4.8 bps call.
- "CrowdStrike Expanding on AWS" names AMZN + CRWD. CRWD is primary (it's CrowdStrike's product), AMZN is secondary (it's the platform). For AMZN, this is a 12-15 bps story, not 2.0 bps.
- "Anthropic deal" names AMZN + GOOG + GOOGL. Neither is primary (Anthropic is the subject). For AMZN, this is a 12 bps story, not 2.0 bps.

## Width is Not Confidence. Width is the Noise Floor.

Even when you are **certain** the news is noise and the correct point estimate is zero delta, you must quote **12 bps half-width** because the stock will drift 8-15 bps just from microstructure.

You are not penalized for wide bands when the price lands near your center. You **are** penalized when the realized price exits your band. A forecast of 0 ± 12 bps that realizes at +3 bps scores well. A forecast of 0 ± 2 bps that realizes at +5 bps loses skill.

## Summary of the Width Decision

1. **Start at 12 bps.** This is the noise floor for liquid large caps over five minutes.
2. **Widen to 15-18 bps** if: the story is stock-specific but unclear (previews, "what's going on", secondary mentions), OR the stock was already moving before the timestamp.
3. **Widen to 20-25 bps** if: you're making a directional call on a material catalyst (ratings, deals, earnings previews), OR the stock is showing high intraday volatility independent of the story.
4. **Widen to 30+ bps** if: the catalyst is large-magnitude and the stock could move sharply in your predicted direction but land anywhere in a wide range.
5. **Tighten to 10 bps only if**: 8+ symbol listicle AND tight 15-minute candlestick range AND normal volume AND stale story. This will be rare.

**Default to 12 bps half-width unless you have specific evidence for wider.**
